A: "The Slaughter-House Cases primarily involved the interpretation of the Fourteenth Amendment and its implications for state citizenship rights, rather than directly addressing civil rights in the modern sense. The case held that the Fourteenth Amendment protects the privileges or immunities of citizenship of the United States, not privileges and immunities of citizenship of a state. While this can be seen as foundational to later civil rights developments, it did not directly address the broad range of civil rights issues that would come to prominence later."

This poem dates back to the 9th or 10th century and celebrates the recapture of Derby, Leicester, Lincoln, Nottingham, and Stamford by King Edmund I in 942."
